$ rp --help
ReplyPen CLI — a thin, scriptable client over the ReplyPen API

Usage:
  rp [command]

Start:
  status      Health summary + recent runs
  ask         Trigger a run from a question and wait for the answer
  run         Inspect and manage the run lifecycle

Manage:
  project     Manage project configuration and resources

Develop:
  dev         Develop and inspect project behavior

Operate:
  fleet       Operate and inspect project health
  admin       Box-level administration (users/projects/catalog; global-admin token)

Local:
  auth        Manage local authentication and inspect access
  self        Manage the rp installation and shell integration

Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

Use "rp [command] --help" for more information about a command.

Health summary + recent runs

      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

$ rp ask --help
Trigger a run from a question and wait for the answer (--no-wait returns the run id).

--replay-chat-run <run id|run link> replays one stored in-app chat turn with its real chat framing, prior turns and principal as a test run (a simulation: no action executes, nothing is placed or committed); the original conversation is untouched. The question becomes optional: given, it replaces that turn's message. Combine with --brain-ref / --mirror-ref to test a fix. Needs the developer grant that `rp run debug` needs on that run. Refuses --principal-*, --file, --scenario, --from, --subject and --session (the replay keeps the turn's own).

Usage:
  rp ask [question] [flags]

Flags:
      --asserted-by string           who asserted the principal (default server-side); requires the --principal-kind/--principal-id pair
      --assurance string             assurance level of the principal assertion (default server-side); requires the --principal-kind/--principal-id pair
      --brain-ref string             run against a non-main brain ref (e.g. dev/refund-rework) — a test run
      --credential-env stringArray   send the local env var NAME as run credential NAME (the value never touches argv); repeatable
      --dry-scope                    resolve and print the principal scope this run WOULD get, without running the agent (no LLM spend)
      --effort string                reasoning effort override: default, pro, or max
      --file stringArray             attach a local file to the synthetic run (repeatable; max 4 files, 5 MiB each, 15 MiB total)
      --from string                  sender address for --scenario email (default "rp-ask@example.test")
      --mailbox string               with --simulation --scenario email: replay this watched mailbox's contract (id, slug or address; default: the scope's single processing mailbox)
      --mirror-ref stringArray       mount <repo>=<ref> (dev/* branch or SHA) of a source mirror for this run only — a test run; repeatable
      --no-wait                      submit and print the run_id immediately, without waiting
      --principal-id string          principal external id (the asserted identity); requires --principal-kind
      --principal-kind string        principal kind to scope the run's data (e.g. kampadmin_person); requires --principal-id
      --replay-chat-run string       replay one stored in-app chat turn (run id or run link) as a side-effect-free test run; the question becomes an optional override
      --scenario string              answer shape: email or raw (default "email")
      --session string               session id to thread the run onto
      --simulation                   dress rehearsal: real brain + grounding, but no action executes, no journal commit and nothing is placed in a mailbox (attachments are refused)
      --subject string               subject for --scenario email (default: compact prompt first line)
      --timeout duration             max time to wait for a terminal status (default 5m0s)

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

$ rp run --help
Inspect and manage the run lifecycle

Usage:
  rp run [flags]
  rp run [command]

Available Commands:
  list           List recent runs (filterable)
  sessions       List chat conversations (sessions) with turns, outcome and feedback
  show           Show one run
  events         Show the full per-event trace
  trace          Show the whole run bundle (--brief: just what was asked and answered)
  debug          Decompose a run into local debug artifacts
  brain-diff     Show the brain commit written by a run
  egress         Show outbound gateway connections and HTTP attempts
  actions        Show the safe action lifecycle history
  guards         Show every security-checkpoint verdict for one run
  thread         Trace one run, provider/local thread, or session through pipeline and placement
  attachments    List or download the files in a chat session
  session        Dump a shared chat session (transcript + its runs) to one markdown file
  feedback       Record score/comment feedback on a run's trace, or mark it processed; `list` reviews rated runs
  retry          Re-run a run; --comment steers the rerun with reviewer guidance (e.g. the corrected answer) and lets it keep the original tier; without it a finished run escalates one tier
  process-thread Process a triage-skipped or security-blocked inbox thread

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

Use "rp run [command] --help" for more information about a command.

$ rp run list --help
List recent runs (filterable)

Usage:
  rp run list [flags]

Flags:
      --before string             cursor: run_id to page to the next (older) page (combines with --days)
      --category string           filter by category (e.g. ok, timeout, cost_cap)
      --days int                  lookback window in days (server default 14, max 90)
      --from string               sender address or name substring (case-insensitive)
      --kind string               filter by kind: email|prompt|mcp|analysis|console|chat
      --learning string[="any"]   filter by learning signal; bare means any, or use =feedback|sent_delta|triage_skipped|triage_corrected
      --limit int                 max runs to return (1..100, server default 50)
      --outcome string            filter by outcome: answered|declined|failed|error|stuck|running|interrupted
      --phone-lab                 list recent phone-lab calls (project developer login; default 20)
      --reviewed                  only runs with a 1–5 human review score (includes held-out eval runs)
      --session string            list every run in this chat session
      --since string              runs created at/after this RFC3339 time or YYYY-MM-DD (UTC); overrides --days
      --subject string            thread subject substring (case-insensitive)
      --until string              runs created before this RFC3339 time, or through this YYYY-MM-DD (UTC)

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

$ rp run sessions --help
List chat conversations, newest first. A conversation is N runs sharing one session id: drill its turns with `rp run thread <session_id>`, then one turn's bodies with `rp run trace <run_id>`. The feedback column is the rollup over the conversation's rated turns; TIER (when served) is the latest turn's depth, `*` = picked by the member; `-o json` is the server payload verbatim, including next_before.

Usage:
  rp run sessions [flags]
  rp run sessions [command]

Available Commands:
  search      Find chat sessions by principal, creation date and prose

Flags:
      --before string    cursor: session_id to page to the next (older) page
      --days int         sessions created in the last N days (max 90; omitted searches all history)
      --kind string      run kind grouped into sessions (server default: chat)
      --limit int        max conversations to return (1..100, server default 50)
      --surface string   filter by chat surface: embed|dashboard|all (server default: all)

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

Use "rp run sessions [command] --help" for more information about a command.

$ rp run sessions search --help
Search authorized chat sessions, newest first, using existing read endpoints. Dates constrain SESSION CREATION, not individual turns or last activity. Date-only values use UTC. --contains matches a case-insensitive literal in title, opening preview or ordinary message text; cards and attachment contents are not searched. Only matching principal/date candidates need a transcript read. Scans at most 20 pages of 100 rows per call, with no history age limit. Coverage and --before continuation are explicit; -o json retains original matching session rows. Read a match with `rp run thread SESSION --transcript`, then `rp run debug RUN`.

Usage:
  rp run sessions search [flags]

Flags:
      --before string           resume an incomplete search with its next_before session ID
      --contains string         case-insensitive literal in session title, opening preview or message prose
      --limit int               max matching sessions to return (1..100) (default 20)
      --principal-id string     exact principal ID (requires --principal-kind)
      --principal-kind string   exact principal kind (requires --principal-id)
      --since string            sessions created at/after RFC3339 or YYYY-MM-DD (UTC)
      --surface string          chat surface: embed|dashboard|all
      --until string            sessions created before RFC3339, or through YYYY-MM-DD (UTC)

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

$ rp run trace --help
Show one run's whole /trace bundle.

Two modes, two questions. `--brief` answers WHAT WAS ASKED AND ANSWERED: question, prior messages, answer/notes, outcome, guards, egress summary — without the system prompt, prompt sections, manifest/bootstrap turns, grounding snapshots, tenant-settings blobs or the event timeline (on a real chat turn that is ~90% of the bytes). Default + `--raw-output` is the DEBUGGING view: everything, JSONL in `-o json`, for `rp run debug`-style forensics.

Usage:
  rp run trace <id|url> [flags]

Flags:
      --brief               reading view: question/answer/outcome only — drops the prompt, grounding, tenant-settings blobs and the event timeline
      --share-token token   share token from a run/share link's ?t= parameter; with it no login is needed
      --stream              stream JSONL to stdout even when it is large

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

$ rp run debug --help
Write a run's trace to .replypen/debug/ as a jq-able JSONL plus a thin markdown index. Phone-lab URLs use your developer login and write an anomaly/turn index, full JSON and event JSONL; bare phone ids need --phone-lab and --project. Accepts a run id (using your login) or a run link someone shared with you (https://app.replypen.com/runs/<id>?t=<token>) — a link needs no `rp auth login` and no profile.

Usage:
  rp run debug <id|url> [flags]

Flags:
      --phone-lab           debug a bare phone-lab run id (requires project scope)
      --share-token token   share token from a run/share link's ?t= parameter; with it no login is needed

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

$ rp run thread --help
Trace a run id, provider conversation id (Gmail thread or Intercom conversation), ReplyPen thread UUID, session id, a pasted Gmail link (print/permalink, #inbox/<id>), or an email address (the newest threads it sent or received — the forwarded-email entry point, incl. triage-skipped mail that never became a run). A run id is resolved through its safe run-status identity first. Shows the pre-agent channel outcome, then every run (newest first) with health, placement, and a deterministic why-no-draft hint. An unknown id is a clean empty answer, not an error. A pasted run link (…/runs/<id>?t=<token>) works with no login.

--transcript flips the view from PIPELINE to CONVERSATION: every turn in reading order (question, answer, outcome, feedback, run link) as compact Markdown, or a {session_id,turns[]} envelope with -o json. It needs a login (a share link carries one run only).

Usage:
  rp run thread <id|url> [flags]

Flags:
      --share-token token   share token from a run/share link's ?t= parameter; with it no login is needed
      --transcript          print the conversation itself (every turn in order: question, answer, outcome, feedback) instead of the pipeline view

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

$ rp run attachments --help
List every file in one chat session (user uploads and assistant-generated, any turn), or download them with --download (user uploads; --include-generated adds assistant files) or --id (repeatable). Name the session by its id or one of its run ids, or with --resource kind:id (e.g. support_ticket:<uuid>) for the session whose action touched that resource. Images download as originally uploaded unless --processed; legacy images exist only as the processed 1536px JPEG. Files stream to .replypen/attachments/<session_id>/ (override with --dir), are verified against the server's sha256 and written 0600. Exits non-zero when any file expired or failed.

Usage:
  rp run attachments [<session_id|run_id>] [flags]

Flags:
      --all-sessions        with --resource: use every linked session instead of requiring exactly one
      --dir string          download directory (default .replypen/attachments/<session_id>)
      --download            download the session's user uploads
      --id stringArray      download only this attachment_id, any origin (repeatable; implies --download)
      --include-generated   with --download: also download assistant-generated files
      --processed           download an image's processed 1536px JPEG instead of its original
      --resource string     find the session by a resource an action touched, as kind:id (e.g. support_ticket:<uuid>)

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

$ rp run session --help
Read a chat session someone shared with you — no login needed, the link is the credential. Paste https://app.replypen.com/s/<token> (or just the token) and rp writes .replypen/debug/session-<session_id>.md: the transcript in reading order, then the runs behind it with a ready-to-run `rp run debug` command per run.

Usage:
  rp run session <share-url|token> [flags]

Flags:
      --share-token token   share token from a run/share link's ?t= parameter; with it no login is needed

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

$ rp run feedback --help
Record a reviewer score (1..5) and/or comment on one run, or set the operator triage state (--processed/--unprocessed/--resolution-note). To review feedback across runs, use `rp run feedback list`.

Usage:
  rp run feedback <run-id> [--score N] [--comment TEXT] [--processed|--unprocessed] [--resolution-note TEXT] [flags]
  rp run feedback [command]

Available Commands:
  list        List runs that received a human score or comment, newest rating first

Flags:
      --comment string           free-text feedback comment
      --processed                mark the run's feedback as processed
      --resolution-note string   operator note on how the feedback was resolved
      --score int                reviewer score 1..5 (chat 👎 = 1, 👍 = 5)
      --unprocessed              mark the run's feedback as not processed

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

Use "rp run feedback [command] --help" for more information about a command.

$ rp run feedback list --help
List runs that received human feedback (a score and/or a comment), newest rating first.

Scores run 1..5 (chat thumbs: 👎 = 1, 👍 = 5); --score negative = 1-2, positive = 4-5, any (default) = every row with a score or comment. The --days/--since/--until window applies to when the feedback was last written (feedback_at), not when the run was created. Comments need review-content access and question/answer previews need project-admin access; below those tiers the cells stay empty.

--processed=false lists what nobody has handled yet, --processed (=true) what was, with its resolution note; mark a row with `rp run feedback <run-id> --processed --resolution-note TEXT`. Project-admin only.

Drill down with `rp run show <run_id>`, and for chat `rp run thread <session_id> --transcript` to read the whole conversation. `-o json` is the server payload verbatim (session_id, previews, next_before included).

Usage:
  rp run feedback list [flags]

Examples:
  rp run feedback list --score negative --kind chat --since 2026-10-16
  rp run feedback list --processed=false -o json
  rp run feedback list --tenant conferentie --days 7 -o json

Flags:
      --before string    cursor: next_before of the previous page (keep the other filters the same)
      --days int         feedback written in the last N days
      --kind string      filter by run kind: email|prompt|mcp|analysis|console|chat
      --limit int        max rows to return (1..100, server default 50)
      --processed        only processed rows (--processed=false: only unhandled ones); omitted = both
      --score string     negative (1-2) | positive (4-5) | any (server default: any)
      --since string     feedback written at/after this RFC3339 time or YYYY-MM-DD (UTC midnight)
      --surface string   chat surface: embed|dashboard|all (server default: all)
      --until string     feedback written before this RFC3339 time, or through this YYYY-MM-DD (UTC)

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

$ rp run retry --help
Re-run a run; --comment steers the rerun with reviewer guidance (e.g. the corrected answer) and lets it keep the original tier; without it a finished run escalates one tier

Usage:
  rp run retry <run-id> [--tier standard|pro] [--comment <text>|--comment-file <path>] [flags]

Flags:
      --comment string        reviewer steering for the rerun, e.g. the corrected answer (server clips at 8 KiB)
      --comment-file string   read the reviewer steering from a file, or - for stdin (server clips at 8 KiB)
      --tier string           model tier for the retry: standard|pro (default: keep the tier with --comment, else escalate one tier)

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

$ rp project --help
Manage project configuration and resources

Usage:
  rp project [flags]
  rp project [command]

Available Commands:
  list            List the projects this token can see (the fleet, for an all-projects token)
  rename          Rename the active project slug and brain repo
  settings        Read, change, and describe project settings
  tenant          Manage tenants (sub-scopes below a project) and their settings
  mailbox         Manage watched mailboxes (the channel plane's inbox watch)
  triage          Read or change mail triage policy and hard rules
  senders         Manage the spam allow/block lists (never-spam / always-spam)
  connection      Manage outbound integration connections
  knowledge       Search knowledge content and configure synchronization
  corpus          Read mailbox exports (harvest/survey/templates)
  database        Manage registered databases (list/read/update + access controls)
  repo            Manage source repos (mirrors + per-repo PR config)
  member          Manage project members
  token           Manage API tokens
  branding        Read or change white-label branding (colours/name/public_base_url)
  env             Manage this project's sealed env secrets
  github          Inspect the GitHub App install for this project
  action-settings Read, change, and probe action-plane configuration
  action          Author action drafts
  chat            Configure, diagnose, and smoke-test embedded chat
  principals      Read or replace the project principal manifest
  egress          Inspect outbound endpoints, volume, and unattributed traffic
  review          Work the review queue: list, show, decide, close or finish cards (like the Review page)

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

Use "rp project [command] --help" for more information about a command.

$ rp project settings proposals --help
Review proposed settings changes (list, approve, reject)

Usage:
  rp project settings proposals [flags]
  rp project settings proposals [command]

Available Commands:
  ls          List settings proposals (pending first, then recently decided)
  show        Show one settings proposal with its payload and evidence
  approve     Approve and apply a settings proposal
  reject      Reject a pending settings proposal

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

Use "rp project settings proposals [command] --help" for more information about a command.

$ rp project tenant budget --help
The limit is recurring: it applies from now on, every month, to every tenant listed under
"shared by". This month's usage is never reset. `set` on a tenant without a limit creates one,
shared with the same tenant in sibling projects. Removing a limit stays with ReplyPen support.

Usage:
  rp project tenant budget [flags]
  rp project tenant budget [command]

Available Commands:
  ls          List every tenant's monthly AI limit and this month's usage
  get         Show a tenant's monthly AI limit and this month's usage
  set         Set (or create) a tenant's monthly AI limit as an absolute USD amount
  add         Raise (or with a negative amount, lower) a tenant's monthly AI limit by USD

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

Use "rp project tenant budget [command] --help" for more information about a command.

      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

$ rp project mailbox --help
Manage watched mailboxes (the channel plane's inbox watch)

Usage:
  rp project mailbox [flags]
  rp project mailbox [command]

Available Commands:
  ls            List watched mailboxes (id, provider, email, status, mode, tenant, health)
  mode          Set the mailbox watch, processing, and delivery mode
  harvest       Start a local-synthesis harvest of a mailbox (optionally wait for the export)
  templates     Export Gmail canned responses as a separate JSON artifact
  imap-env      Write an IMAP mailbox env file for local deep harvest (0600; values never printed)
  settings      Read or edit nested mailbox settings (persona/channel)
  connect       Print the dashboard Connections URL to start a provider's browser OAuth
  connect-imap  Connect a generic IMAP/SMTP mailbox (live-probed before it's saved)
  seed-imap     Store an IMAP mailbox's server settings without a password and print its no-login password link
  password-link Print the no-login password link for an IMAP mailbox (also used for rotation)
  test          Re-run the live IMAP/SMTP check on a connected mailbox and print the checklist

$ rp project mailbox harvest --help

Usage:

Flags:
      --clean               request the cleaned corpus (server default true) (default true)
      --lookback-days int   only harvest the last N days of sent history (0 = server default, max HARVEST_LOOKBACK_DAYS)

      --timeout duration    max time to wait under --wait (default 5m0s)

$ rp project mailbox connect-imap --help
Link a generic IMAP/SMTP mailbox to a project (or tenant with --tenant). The server logs in over IMAP, selects INBOX, and authenticates SMTP before persisting anything — a failure saves nothing.

The password is read from $RC_MAILBOX_PASSWORD or, if unset, prompted on stdin — never passed as an argument. Defaults mirror the server: --username defaults to --email, --smtp-host to --imap-host, and ports/TLS default to 993/implicit (IMAP) and 587/starttls (SMTP).

Don't have the password? Use `rp project mailbox seed-imap` instead and send the customer the no-login link it prints.

Usage:
  rp project mailbox connect-imap --email <addr> --imap-host <host> [flags]

Flags:
      --email string           mailbox email address (required)
      --imap-host string       IMAP server host (required)
      --imap-port int          IMAP server port (default: server 993)
      --imap-tls string        IMAP TLS mode: none|starttls|implicit (default: server implicit)
      --smtp-host string       SMTP server host (default: --imap-host)
      --smtp-password-prompt   prompt for a separate SMTP password (else $RC_MAILBOX_SMTP_PASSWORD, else the IMAP password)
      --smtp-port int          SMTP server port (default: server 587)
      --smtp-tls string        SMTP TLS mode: none|starttls|implicit (default: server starttls)
      --smtp-username string   SMTP username override (default: --username)
      --username string        IMAP/SMTP login username (default: --email)

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

$ rp project mailbox seed-imap --help
Save everything about a generic IMAP/SMTP mailbox except the password, then print a stable, no-login link to send to whoever holds that password.

Nothing is probed yet — there is no credential to authenticate with. The mailbox sits in awaiting_credential (never polled, so it cannot fail in the background) until someone opens the link and enters the password; it goes live automatically when that password passes the live check.

The link does not expire and is reusable: the same URL is how the customer rotates the password later. It can only SET the password for this one mailbox — never read one, and never reach another mailbox. Use `rp project mailbox password-link <id>` to print it again.

Usage:
  rp project mailbox seed-imap --email <addr> --imap-host <host> [flags]

Flags:
      --email string           mailbox email address (required)
      --imap-host string       IMAP server host (required)
      --imap-port int          IMAP server port (default: server 993)
      --imap-tls string        IMAP TLS mode: none|starttls|implicit (default: server implicit)
      --smtp-host string       SMTP server host (default: --imap-host)
      --smtp-port int          SMTP server port (default: server 587)
      --smtp-tls string        SMTP TLS mode: none|starttls|implicit (default: server starttls)
      --smtp-username string   SMTP username override (default: --username)
      --username string        IMAP/SMTP login username (default: --email)

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

Use "rp project triage rules [command] --help" for more information about a command.

$ rp project triage eval --help
Reads every case in the brain's _internal/triage/cases/ at --cases-ref (default HEAD, the synced main), classifies each through the server's real triage path (prefilter, live policy + rules, the triage docs of --brain-ref, persona language, email-type gate, model route) and scores it against the case's expected outcome. Nothing is ingested, drafted or sent; it spends one model call per case and --repeat. --baseline <ref> runs that ref first and grades both on the baseline's exact cases commit. It does not measure the main agent (active-session follow-ups skip triage).

Usage:
  rp project triage eval [flags]

Examples:
  rp project triage eval --brain-ref dev/shorter-triage --baseline HEAD --repeat 2

Flags:
      --baseline string    also run this brain ref first and show which cases changed class
      --brain-ref string   brain revision whose triage docs to test: HEAD (default), stable, edge, a commit SHA or a dev/* branch
      --cases-ref string   brain revision to read the cases (answer key) from (default HEAD)
      --mailbox string     mailbox id: adds its mailbox-scoped policy, rules and persona language
      --only string        comma-separated case ids to run
      --repeat int         decisions per case (1-3): a case passes only when every attempt agrees (default 1)

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

$ rp project knowledge content search --help
Search KB articles and write matched articles to local artifacts

Usage:
  rp project knowledge content search <query> [flags]

Flags:
      --limit int         max matched articles to materialize (default 25)
      --no-materialize    skip writing full article markdown files
      --out string        artifact directory (must not already exist)
      --provider string   KB provider mount under /kb (default: all mounted providers)

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

$ rp project knowledge article --help
Write and read help-centre articles from a markdown block.

The unit is one file: `replypen: helpcenter/v1` YAML front matter (op: create|update,
provider, id or collection_id, title, optional keywords/anchor) + the article body in markdown.
A write lands in the provider's DRAFT lane unless you pass --publish, and `apply --dry-run`
first prints the exact provider calls. `get` hands you an editable block for a live article.
The project needs a help-centre write grant (once, by an owner):
  rp project connection add integration_key=<helpscout_docs|intercom|knowledgeowl> \
    label=help-center token=…

Usage:
  rp project knowledge article [flags]
  rp project knowledge article [command]

Available Commands:
  apply       Apply a help-centre article block to its provider
  get         Fetch one help-centre article as an applyable markdown block

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

Use "rp project knowledge article [command] --help" for more information about a command.

$ rp project knowledge article apply --help
Apply a help-centre article block (`replypen: helpcenter/v1`) to its provider.

Run it with --dry-run first: the output is the exact provider calls that would be sent, in full.
A published article is refused unless you pass --publish; without it the change lands in the
provider's draft lane and the live article stays untouched. --from - reads the block from stdin.
A real write needs the `knowledge:write` token scope; --dry-run and `article get` work with read.

Example block:
  ---
  replypen: helpcenter/v1
  op: update
  provider: helpscout
  id: "1234"
  title: Refunds within 14 days
  ---
  Body markdown; it replaces the whole article.

Usage:
  rp project knowledge article apply --from <file.md> [flags]

Flags:
      --dry-run       show the provider calls without sending them
      --from string   markdown file holding the article block (- reads stdin)
      --publish       allow changing a published (live) article

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

$ rp project knowledge article get --help
Fetch one help-centre article as an applyable markdown block.

The output is a ready `replypen: helpcenter/v1` block with `op: update` and the article's
current title/ids in the front matter: edit the body (or the title) and feed the same file
back to `rp project knowledge article apply --from FILE.md --dry-run`.
--id is the provider-native article id (the id in the article's help-centre URL or in the
synced KB file's front matter), never our internal document id.
Use --out FILE to write the block straight to disk instead of stdout. Reading works with the
ordinary read scope; only the apply write needs `knowledge:write`.

Usage:
  rp project knowledge article get --provider <provider> --id <id> [flags]

Flags:
      --id string         provider-native article id
      --out string        write the block to this file instead of stdout
      --provider string   help-centre provider (helpscout, intercom, knowledgeowl)

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. $ rp project knowledge summaries apply --help
Replace synced KB article summaries with reviewed ones.

--from is JSON: {"patches": [{"provider", "id", "body_hash", "summary"}, …]} (or a bare array),
with id and body_hash copied from the article's synced frontmatter. Each patch is checked: ok,
stale (the article changed since you judged it), not_found or invalid (one line, ≤200 chars).
The ok ones are applied by a KB sync queued for this scope; later syncs keep them until the
article body changes. --tenant targets the tenant's own KB. A real apply needs the
`knowledge:write` token scope; --dry-run works with read.

Usage:
  rp project knowledge summaries apply --from <patches.json> [flags]

Flags:
      --dry-run       check the patches without queueing the sync
      --from string   JSON patch file

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

$ rp project knowledge sync run --help
Queue a sync of this project's knowledge sources into /kb now, instead of waiting for the
periodic sweep (daily; site-published bundles hourly). Syncs the project's own KB; --tenant syncs
that tenant's own KB instead. A sync already queued absorbs the request; one already running gets
a follow-up sync, so a change made just now is always picked up.

--wait polls until the sync finishes and prints the article total and the article files that
changed; it exits non-zero when the sync failed or the knowledge source could not be read.
Needs the `knowledge:write` (or config:write) token scope.

Usage:
  rp project knowledge sync run [--wait] [flags]

Flags:
      --timeout duration   max time to wait under --wait (default 15m0s)
      --wait               poll until the sync finishes and print what changed

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

$ rp project database --help
Manage registered databases (list/read/update + access controls).

There is no "database add": a database EXISTS because a sealed grounding env key named
<PROJECT>_<DBKEY>_DSN exists. To register one (read-only DB role; the ReplyPen box must be
network-allowlisted by your DB provider):

  printf %s "$DSN" | rp project env set key=<PROJECT>_<DBKEY>_DSN

<dbkey> lowercased is the db= short name brain scripts use: PROBACKUP_BACKUPS_DSN <-> lib.db(db="backups").
Verify the DSN env actually resolves with `rp dev console database list`.

`ls` here lists the DSNs this project has CONFIGURED (description, scope manifest, or PII columns) —
a brand-new DSN shows up once you annotate it: `rp project database set <PROJECT>_<DBKEY>_DSN description=…`.

`*_WRITE_DSN` keys are the action/write plane (--plane action), not grounding databases.

Usage:
  rp project database [flags]
  rp project database [command]

Available Commands:
  ls          List databases
  get         Show one database
  set         Update a database
  controls    Read or change a database's access controls
  preview     Preview the scoped rows a (tenant, principal) would see

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

Use "rp project database [command] --help" for more information about a command.

$ rp project token ls --help
List tokens, least recently used first. Active tokens only unless --include-revoked. "never" means no use
since last-used tracking began (2026-10-12). Use --unused-days N to list stale credentials worth revoking.
-o json keeps the server's raw items (scope, user agent, …) after the same filter and sort.
--all lists machine credentials across EVERY project with their owner (all-projects global-admin token);
with --include-revoked it adds revoked, expired and superseded ones that died in the last 30 days.

Usage:
  rp project token ls [flags]

Flags:
      --all               machine credentials across every project, with owner (all-projects global-admin token)
      --include-revoked   include revoked tokens (default: active only; with --all: revoked/expired/superseded in the last 30 days)
      --unused-days int   only tokens never used or last used more than N days ago
      --wide              add SCOPE and USER AGENT columns (with --all: ID and the full user agent)

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

$ rp project env --help
Manage this project's sealed env secrets.

A grounding key named `<PROJECT>_<DBKEY>_DSN` auto-registers a database (see `rp project database --help`);
`*_WRITE_DSN` belongs on --plane action.

Usage:
  rp project env [flags]
  rp project env [command]

Available Commands:
  pull        Fetch the production grounding env and write ./.env (0600); prints NAMES + count, never values
  diff        Compare local ./.env to the server (NAMES-only drift); nonzero exit on drift
  keys        List grounding env key NAMES (log-safe, no values); --tenant lists the tenant's own
  set         Upsert one env var (value from STDIN by default; never echoed)
  rm          Delete one env var
  reveal      Print one env var's value (sensitive, shown once)

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

Use "rp project env [command] --help" for more information about a command.

      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

Use "rp project action draft [command] --help" for more information about a command.

$ rp project chat --help
Configure, diagnose, and smoke-test embedded chat

Usage:
  rp project chat [flags]
  rp project chat [command]

Available Commands:
  get         Show current values (value / effective / default)
  set         Change values (sparse, validate-then-apply server-side)
  secret      Manage the dedicated chat signing secret
  token       Mint a five-minute server chat token
  send        Send one chat turn and print its SSE frames and session + run IDs
  replay      Replay a file of user messages as one chat session and tabulate tier, time and answer per turn
  session     Print a session's persisted transcript as the widget would reopen it
  decide      Settle a chat card (e.g. publish or decline a website change) and print its projection
  card        Print a chat card's current projection (--wait polls until it settles)
  follow      Stream a still-running turn (e.g. a card's review_run_id) like send
  attachment  Download one file of your own conversation (e.g. a review screenshot)
  doctor      Diagnose embedded-chat configuration and recent rejects
  brief       Print the secret-free chat implementation brief

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

Use "rp project chat [command] --help" for more information about a command.

$ rp project chat replay --help
One user message per non-empty line; lines starting with # are skipped and a line `---` starts a fresh session for the lines after it. Turns run sequentially, each waiting for its answer. --tier is the initial pick, sent only on a session's first turn; later turns let the host route. `-o json` prints the array of turns.

Usage:
  rp project chat replay <file> [flags]

Flags:
      --action string        action slug for --intent action_modify
      --integration string   connector key for --intent integration_setup (e.g. clickup)
      --intent string        seed the new --lane setup conversation like chat/new?intent=…: integration_setup, action_create, action_modify
      --lane string          dashboard lane over the OAuth login instead of an embed token: setup (Set up & improve) or data (Ask your data)
      --origin string        embedding-page origin (default: token origin claim)
      --session string       continue this existing session (until the first ---)
      --tier string          session tier for a new --lane conversation: standard (Quick) or pro (Deeper)
      --token string         embed chat token (default: RC_CHAT_TOKEN)

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

Usage:

Flags:
      --file string     write the file here (default ./<attachment-id>)
      --lane string     dashboard lane over the OAuth login instead of an embed token: setup (Set up & improve) or data (Ask your data)
      --origin string   embedding-page origin (default: token origin claim)
      --token string    embed chat token (default: RC_CHAT_TOKEN)

Global Flags:
      --no-preview       write large output artifacts but omit head/tail previews
      --out-dir string   directory for large output artifacts (default: RC_OUTPUT_DIR or .replypen/output)
  -o, --output string    output format: json|table (default: auto-detect)
      --profile string   token profile to use (default: RC_PROFILE, else auto — the brain in the current directory, else "default")
      --project string   scope the request to one project by name or id (default: RC_PROJECT; requires an all-projects token, unless the checkout's .replypen.toml binds that project via [[also]])
      --raw-output       disable progressive output spill and print full payloads to stdout
      --scope string     force request routing: project|tenant. "project" clears any resolved tenant (--tenant, a brain checkout, or a tenant-bound login) so a tenant-capable command hits the project route; "tenant" requires a resolvable tenant. With a tenant-pinned token, --scope project routes to the project and the server returns 403 — the flag controls routing, not authorization.
      --tenant string    override the login tenant where supported (default: RC_TENANT)

Usage:
  rp project review [flags]
  rp project review [command]

  close       Close a queue card already handled elsewhere, with a note saying where (commits, thread)

$ rp project review decide --help
Records the decision exactly like the Review page. A decided queue card is picked up by the
review-implement run (Accept/Noise/Later close there; other labels become work). If the issue is
already fixed elsewhere, use `close` instead so no duplicate worker starts.
